I haven’t really paid attention to the speed of it. It doesn’t feel like I’m, you know, shoveling food down or anything… but maybe I’m eating faster than I normally would. I’d have to notice it more, if that’s okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost control over your eating during those times when you eat more than you planned?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t love how that sounds, but on those evenings, once I start, it really can feel like I’m kind of on autopilot.\n\nLike I’ll be telling myself, “Just stop, you’ve got an early crew start tomorrow,” and then I still end up back in the kitchen getting more. So… yes, I guess that does feel like losing control, at least in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s kind of what made me bring it up.\n\nAfter a really long day on site, or sometimes after a neighborhood meeting, I’ll get home and just… eat way more than I meant to. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s kind of what made me bring it up.\n\nAfter a really long day on site, or sometimes after a neighborhood meeting, I’ll get home and just… eat way more than I meant to. Like leftover takeout, cereal, chips, whatever’s around. And it can happen pretty quickly, I guess, enough that I end up uncomfortably full. Is that what you mean by a large amount?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed of yourself after these episodes of eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it ashamed exactly. I mean, I definitely don’t feel great afterward. I usually feel kind of embarrassed with myself, like, “Why did I do that?” But I also try not to make a huge deal out of it, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess… maybe a little ashamed, but I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of feeling like you're on autopilot while eating happen often?\n\nPatient: Um… often enough that I probably shouldn’t pretend it’s rare.\n\nIf I look back over the last few months, I’d say it averages out to about once a week. I’d have to notice it more, if that’s okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost control over your eating during those times when you eat more than you planned?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t love how that sounds, but on those evenings, once I start, it really can feel like I’m kind of on autopilot.\n\nLike I’ll be telling myself, “Just stop, you’ve got an early crew start tomorrow,” and then I still end up back in the kitchen getting more. So… yes, I guess that does feel like losing control, at least in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s kind of what made me bring it up.\n\nAfter a really long day on site, or sometimes after a neighborhood meeting, I’ll get home and just… eat way more than I meant to. Like leftover takeout, cereal, chips, whatever’s around. And it can happen pretty quickly, I guess, enough that I end up uncomfortably full. Is that what you mean by a large amount?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed of yourself after these episodes of eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it ashamed exactly.
